{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01D2eLvLJZLfp8cpHGenTZ1w","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * The migration set a service ACTUALLY APPLIES — the single answer to a question\n3\t * eight invariants were each answering for themselves, four of them wrongly.\n4\t *\n5\t * # The defect this exists to remove\n6\t *\n7\t * On 2026-05-18 four services squashed their migrations into a `Baseline` and\n8\t * moved the originals to `src/…/migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/`. The archive is\n9\t * dead by construction: every service's TypeORM entry declares\n10\t * `migrations: ['src/…/migrations/[0-9]*…']`, a pattern that neither matches a\n11\t * dot-directory nor descends into one. Fifty-five files stopped running.\n12\t *\n13\t * Invariants that read migrations did not all notice. The idiom\n14\t *\n15\t *     git ls-files 'apps/<svc>/src/migrations/*.ts'\n16\t *\n17\t * looks scoped to one directory and is not: a git pathspec `*` crosses `/`, so\n18\t * it returns the archive too — 32 files for auth-service, 13 of them dead. So\n19\t * `auth-users-tenant-fk.spec.ts` asserted that a FOREIGN KEY exists, PASSED, and\n20\t * was reading an archived migration. The constraint it guards is absent from\n21\t * every migration the runtime applies, and so is `shared.access_logs`, which\n22\t * four other layers still declare and request middleware still writes to.\n23\t * ORPHAN-CRITICAL-516.\n24\t *\n25\t * A gate reporting green off evidence the runtime never executes is worse than\n26\t * no gate: it is a standing claim that something is protected when it is not.\n27\t *\n28\t * # Why a shared module rather than a fix in each spec\n29\t *\n30\t * Four of the eight migration-reading specs already guard against the archive,\n31\t * each in its own way, and `audit-immutability-triggers.spec.ts` even has a\n32\t * private function called `loadMigrationCorpus`. The right pattern was in the\n33\t * repository and half the callers did not use it — the ORPHAN-HIGH-499 shape.\n34\t * Eight private answers to one question is how four of them drift.\n35\t *\n36\t * # Why the directory is DERIVED\n37\t *\n38\t * Services disagree about where migrations live (`src/migrations` for auth,\n39\t * admin, event-store, messaging; `src/database/migrations` for the other ten)\n40\t * and about the glob's suffix spelling. Hardcoding either would put this\n41\t * module's notion of \"effective\" and the runtime's in two places, which is the\n42\t * defect one layer up. `data-source.ts` is the per-service TypeORM entry point\n43\t * CLAUDE.md names as the migration SSoT, and it declares the pattern uniformly,\n44\t * so that is what is read.\n45\t *\n46\t * # What this module does NOT own\n47\t *\n48\t * Whether a service's runtime array actually REGISTERS every file on disk —\n49\t * farm-service uses an explicit array rather than a glob, and\n50\t * `migration-registration-completeness.spec.ts` owns that question. This module\n51\t * answers only \"what is on disk and not retired\", which is the input that spec\n52\t * and the seven others need.\n53\t */\n54\t\n55\timport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\n56\timport { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\n57\timport { basename, dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';\n58\t\n59\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..');\n60\t\n61\t/** `migrations: ['src/<something>/[0-9]*…']` in a service's TypeORM entry. */\n62\tconst MIGRATIONS_GLOB = /migrations:\\s*\\[\\s*'(src\\/[A-Za-z0-9_/-]*migrations)\\/\\[0-9\\]\\*/;\n63\t\n64\texport interface MigrationCorpus {\n65\t  /** Service directory name, e.g. `auth-service`. */\n66\t  readonly service: string;\n67\t  /** Repo-relative migrations directory, derived from the service's own entry. */\n68\t  readonly directory: string;\n69\t  /** Repo-relative paths of the migrations the runtime applies, sorted. */\n70\t  readonly files: readonly string[];\n71\t  /** Repo-relative paths of migrations the runtime no longer applies. */\n72\t  readonly archived: readonly string[];\n73\t  /** Concatenated source of the effective set — what a fresh database gets. */\n74\t  readonly source: string;\n75\t}\n76\t\n77\tfunction gitFiles(pathspec: string): string[] {\n78\t  return execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', pathspec], { encoding: 'utf8' })\n79\t    .split('\\n')\n80\t    .filter(Boolean);\n81\t}\n82\t\n83\t/**\n84\t * The migrations directory `service` declares, read from its TypeORM entry.\n85\t *\n86\t * Refuses rather than guessing. A service whose entry this cannot parse would\n87\t * otherwise be modelled against the wrong directory — reporting files it does\n88\t * not run, or omitting files it does — which is exactly the error class this\n89\t * module exists to remove.\n90\t */\n91\tfunction migrationsDirectory(service: string): string {\n92\t  const candidates = [\n93\t    `apps/${service}/src/database/data-source.ts`,\n94\t    `apps/${service}/src/data-source.ts`,\n95\t  ].filter((rel) => existsSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel)));\n96\t\n97\t  for (const rel of candidates) {\n98\t    const match = MIGRATIONS_GLOB.exec(readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8'));\n99\t    if (match?.[1]) return `apps/${service}/${match[1]}`;\n100\t  }\n101\t  throw new Error(\n102\t    `migration-corpus: cannot derive the migrations directory for ${service}. ` +\n103\t      `Looked for a \\`migrations: ['src/…/[0-9]*…']\\` glob in ` +\n104\t      `${candidates.join(', ') || '(no data-source.ts found)'}. This module refuses ` +\n105\t      'to guess: a wrong directory would silently change which migrations count ' +\n106\t      'as effective, which is the defect it exists to prevent.',\n107\t  );\n108\t}\n109\t\n110\t/**\n111\t * True when `rel` is a migration the runtime applies.\n112\t *\n113\t * Mirrors `[0-9]*` exactly: the file sits DIRECTLY in the declared directory and\n114\t * its name begins with a digit. A file under `.archive/` fails the first clause,\n115\t * which is precisely why it is dead — and why a `git ls-files` pathspec, whose\n116\t * `*` crosses `/`, is the wrong instrument for this question.\n117\t */\n118\tfunction isEffective(rel: string, directory: string): boolean {\n119\t  if (dirname(rel) !== directory) return false;\n120\t  const name = basename(rel);\n121\t  return /^[0-9]/.test(name) && (name.endsWith('.ts') || name.endsWith('.js'));\n122\t}\n123\t\n124\tconst cache = new Map<string, MigrationCorpus>();\n125\t\n126\t/** The migrations `service` applies, and the ones it has retired. */\n127\texport function migrationCorpus(service: string): MigrationCorpus {\n128\t  const cached = cache.get(service);\n129\t  if (cached) return cached;\n130\t\n131\t  const directory = migrationsDirectory(service);\n132\t\n133\t  // Deliberately the archive-matching pathspec, so the split is COMPUTED here\n134\t  // rather than assumed. Asking git for only the effective files would make the\n135\t  // `archived` list unknowable, and that list is what proves the split is real.\n136\t  const all = gitFiles(`${directory}/*.ts`);\n137\t  const files = all.filter((rel) => isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n138\t  const archived = all.filter((rel) => !isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n139\t\n140\t  if (files.length === 0) {\n141\t    throw new Error(\n142\t      `migration-corpus: ${service} has no effective migrations under ${directory}. ` +\n143\t        'Either the service name is wrong or every migration has been archived, ' +\n144\t        'and a spec asserting over an empty corpus would pass vacuously.',\n145\t    );\n146\t  }\n147\t\n148\t  const corpus: MigrationCorpus = {\n149\t    service,\n150\t    directory,\n151\t    files,\n152\t    archived,\n153\t    source: files\n154\t      .map((rel) => `-- ${rel}\\n${readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8')}`)\n155\t      .join('\\n'),\n156\t  };\n157\t  cache.set(service, corpus);\n158\t  return corpus;\n159\t}\n160\t\n161\t/** The concatenated effective source of several services, in the order given. */\n162\texport function migrationSource(...services: readonly string[]): string {\n163\t  return services.map((service) => migrationCorpus(service).source).join('\\n---\\n');\n164\t}\n165\t\n166\t/**\n167\t * Every service that owns a migrations directory.\n168\t *\n169\t * `:(glob)` is load-bearing: without the pathspec magic git treats `**` as\n170\t * two ordinary `*`, which cannot match ZERO directories, so services whose\n171\t * migrations live directly under `src/migrations` (admin-api, auth) silently\n172\t * dropped out of the fleet while `src/database/migrations` services stayed in.\n173\t */\n174\texport function servicesWithMigrations(): string[] {\n175\t  const services = new Set<string>();\n176\t  for (const rel of gitFiles(':(glob)apps/*/src/**/migrations/*.ts')) {\n177\t    const service = rel.split('/')[1];\n178\t    if (service) services.add(service);\n179\t  }\n180\t  return [...services].sort();\n181\t}\n182\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9842d2e2-2674-4c40-84ec-bcd1c4474f20","uuid":"7b65d092-ecbf-42bf-9eb3-2d2642045001","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:27:11.542Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a29dcc8b0754/tests/invariants/lib/migration-corpus.ts","content":"/**\n * The migration set a service ACTUALLY APPLIES — the single answer to a question\n * eight invariants were each answering for themselves, four of them wrongly.\n *\n * # The defect this exists to remove\n *\n * On 2026-05-18 four services squashed their migrations into a `Baseline` and\n * moved the originals to `src/…/migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/`. The archive is\n * dead by construction: every service's TypeORM entry declares\n * `migrations: ['src/…/migrations/[0-9]*…']`, a pattern that neither matches a\n * dot-directory nor descends into one. Fifty-five files stopped running.\n *\n * Invariants that read migrations did not all notice. The idiom\n *\n *     git ls-files 'apps/<svc>/src/migrations/*.ts'\n *\n * looks scoped to one directory and is not: a git pathspec `*` crosses `/`, so\n * it returns the archive too — 32 files for auth-service, 13 of them dead. So\n * `auth-users-tenant-fk.spec.ts` asserted that a FOREIGN KEY exists, PASSED, and\n * was reading an archived migration. The constraint it guards is absent from\n * every migration the runtime applies, and so is `shared.access_logs`, which\n * four other layers still declare and request middleware still writes to.\n * ORPHAN-CRITICAL-516.\n *\n * A gate reporting green off evidence the runtime never executes is worse than\n * no gate: it is a standing claim that something is protected when it is not.\n *\n * # Why a shared module rather than a fix in each spec\n *\n * Four of the eight migration-reading specs already guard against the archive,\n * each in its own way, and `audit-immutability-triggers.spec.ts` even has a\n * private function called `loadMigrationCorpus`. The right pattern was in the\n * repository and half the callers did not use it — the ORPHAN-HIGH-499 shape.\n * Eight private answers to one question is how four of them drift.\n *\n * # Why the directory is DERIVED\n *\n * Services disagree about where migrations live (`src/migrations` for auth,\n * admin, event-store, messaging; `src/database/migrations` for the other ten)\n * and about the glob's suffix spelling. Hardcoding either would put this\n * module's notion of \"effective\" and the runtime's in two places, which is the\n * defect one layer up. `data-source.ts` is the per-service TypeORM entry point\n * CLAUDE.md names as the migration SSoT, and it declares the pattern uniformly,\n * so that is what is read.\n *\n * # What this module does NOT own\n *\n * Whether a service's runtime array actually REGISTERS every file on disk —\n * farm-service uses an explicit array rather than a glob, and\n * `migration-registration-completeness.spec.ts` owns that question. This module\n * answers only \"what is on disk and not retired\", which is the input that spec\n * and the seven others need.\n */\n\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { basename, dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..');\n\n/** `migrations: ['src/<something>/[0-9]*…']` in a service's TypeORM entry. */\nconst MIGRATIONS_GLOB = /migrations:\\s*\\[\\s*'(src\\/[A-Za-z0-9_/-]*migrations)\\/\\[0-9\\]\\*/;\n\nexport interface MigrationCorpus {\n  /** Service directory name, e.g. `auth-service`. */\n  readonly service: string;\n  /** Repo-relative migrations directory, derived from the service's own entry. */\n  readonly directory: string;\n  /** Repo-relative paths of the migrations the runtime applies, sorted. */\n  readonly files: readonly string[];\n  /** Repo-relative paths of migrations the runtime no longer applies. */\n  readonly archived: readonly string[];\n  /** Concatenated source of the effective set — what a fresh database gets. */\n  readonly source: string;\n}\n\nfunction gitFiles(pathspec: string): string[] {\n  return execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', pathspec], { encoding: 'utf8' })\n    .split('\\n')\n    .filter(Boolean);\n}\n\n/**\n * The migrations directory `service` declares, read from its TypeORM entry.\n *\n * Refuses rather than guessing. A service whose entry this cannot parse would\n * otherwise be modelled against the wrong directory — reporting files it does\n * not run, or omitting files it does — which is exactly the error class this\n * module exists to remove.\n */\nfunction migrationsDirectory(service: string): string {\n  const candidates = [\n    `apps/${service}/src/database/data-source.ts`,\n    `apps/${service}/src/data-source.ts`,\n  ].filter((rel) => existsSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel)));\n\n  for (const rel of candidates) {\n    const match = MIGRATIONS_GLOB.exec(readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8'));\n    if (match?.[1]) return `apps/${service}/${match[1]}`;\n  }\n  throw new Error(\n    `migration-corpus: cannot derive the migrations directory for ${service}. ` +\n      `Looked for a \\`migrations: ['src/…/[0-9]*…']\\` glob in ` +\n      `${candidates.join(', ') || '(no data-source.ts found)'}. This module refuses ` +\n      'to guess: a wrong directory would silently change which migrations count ' +\n      'as effective, which is the defect it exists to prevent.',\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * True when `rel` is a migration the runtime applies.\n *\n * Mirrors `[0-9]*` exactly: the file sits DIRECTLY in the declared directory and\n * its name begins with a digit. A file under `.archive/` fails the first clause,\n * which is precisely why it is dead — and why a `git ls-files` pathspec, whose\n * `*` crosses `/`, is the wrong instrument for this question.\n */\nfunction isEffective(rel: string, directory: string): boolean {\n  if (dirname(rel) !== directory) return false;\n  const name = basename(rel);\n  return /^[0-9]/.test(name) && (name.endsWith('.ts') || name.endsWith('.js'));\n}\n\nconst cache = new Map<string, MigrationCorpus>();\n\n/** The migrations `service` applies, and the ones it has retired. */\nexport function migrationCorpus(service: string): MigrationCorpus {\n  const cached = cache.get(service);\n  if (cached) return cached;\n\n  const directory = migrationsDirectory(service);\n\n  // Deliberately the archive-matching pathspec, so the split is COMPUTED here\n  // rather than assumed. Asking git for only the effective files would make the\n  // `archived` list unknowable, and that list is what proves the split is real.\n  const all = gitFiles(`${directory}/*.ts`);\n  const files = all.filter((rel) => isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n  const archived = all.filter((rel) => !isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n\n  if (files.length === 0) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `migration-corpus: ${service} has no effective migrations under ${directory}. ` +\n        'Either the service name is wrong or every migration has been archived, ' +\n        'and a spec asserting over an empty corpus would pass vacuously.',\n    );\n  }\n\n  const corpus: MigrationCorpus = {\n    service,\n    directory,\n    files,\n    archived,\n    source: files\n      .map((rel) => `-- ${rel}\\n${readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8')}`)\n      .join('\\n'),\n  };\n  cache.set(service, corpus);\n  return corpus;\n}\n\n/** The concatenated effective source of several services, in the order given. */\nexport function migrationSource(...services: readonly string[]): string {\n  return services.map((service) => migrationCorpus(service).source).join('\\n---\\n');\n}\n\n/**\n * Every service that owns a migrations directory.\n *\n * `:(glob)` is load-bearing: without the pathspec magic git treats `**` as\n * two ordinary `*`, which cannot match ZERO directories, so services whose\n * migrations live directly under `src/migrations` (admin-api, auth) silently\n * dropped out of the fleet while `src/database/migrations` services stayed in.\n */\nexport function servicesWithMigrations(): string[] {\n  const services = new Set<string>();\n  for (const rel of gitFiles(':(glob)apps/*/src/**/migrations/*.ts')) {\n    const service = rel.split('/')[1];\n    if (service) services.add(service);\n  }\n  return [...services].sort();\n}\n","numLines":182,"startLine":1,"totalLines":182}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GBEnotPjw5mcCj2BbYeHwe","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Finance currency SSoT invariant (FARM-MEDIUM-145, FARM-HIGH-151, HR-HIGH-008).\n3\t * ============================================================================\n4\t *\n5\t * The tenant default currency has exactly ONE source of truth:\n6\t *   - farm-service: `finance_settings.defaultCurrency`, resolved through\n7\t *     `FinanceSettingsService`;\n8\t *   - hr-service: `hr_payroll_cost_settings.defaultCurrency`, resolved\n9\t *     through `PayrollCostSettingsService` (itself projected from the\n10\t *     farm SSoT via the `FinanceSettingsUpdated` event).\n11\t *\n12\t * The bug this freezes: independent hardcoded ISO-currency literals drift.\n13\t * `feeds`/`equipment` defaulted to `'TRY'`, `create-feeding-record.handler.ts`\n14\t * to `'NOK'`, HR `employee.entity.ts` to `'USD'` — so a tenant recording feed\n15\t * in one form and equipment in another silently booked in different currencies.\n16\t *\n17\t * # Why this is a rule and not a list\n18\t *\n19\t * It used to guard `FINANCE_HANDLER_ROOTS` recursively PLUS a hardcoded\n20\t * `NAMED_GUARDED_FILES` array, so a currency writer outside those roots was\n21\t * covered only if somebody remembered to add it. Nobody had to: the docblock\n22\t * claimed \"there is no longer a hardcoded-currency create-handler outside this\n23\t * guarded set\" and running the spec's own pattern over every\n24\t * `apps/**\\/*.handler.ts` found SEVEN — two hr payroll handlers (HR-HIGH-008,\n25\t * a real defect: `|| 'USD'` against a platform default of NOK, stamped onto\n26\t * the cross-service `PayrollProcessed` event) and five billing handlers.\n27\t * A ratchet whose coverage is a memo is not a ratchet.\n28\t *\n29\t * So the guard now scans EVERY handler and EVERY entity, and a currency\n30\t * literal is a failure unless it appears in {@link DECLARED_EXEMPTIONS} with a\n31\t * ledger finding behind it. The exemption list can only shrink: a stale entry\n32\t * (declared but no longer matching) fails, so removing the literal forces\n33\t * removing the entry rather than leaving a permanent hole.\n34\t *\n35\t * # When this spec fails\n36\t *\n37\t *   1. A handler or entity grew a `|| 'XXX'` / `?? 'XXX'` / `currency: 'XXX'`\n38\t *      / `default: 'XXX'` currency literal. Resolve it through\n39\t *      `FinanceSettingsService.getDefaultCurrencyInTx(...)` /\n40\t *      `PayrollCostSettingsService.getDefaultCurrencyInTx(...)` instead. For a\n41\t *      column default there is no correct literal at all — a default cannot\n42\t *      know the tenant — so drop it and let the NOT NULL column force the\n43\t *      writer to supply one (precedent: migration 1802200000000).\n44\t *   2. A declared exemption no longer matches. Delete the entry; the debt it\n45\t *      recorded is paid.\n46\t *   3. An exemption cites a finding the ledger has RESOLVED. The finding says\n47\t *      the work is done, so the exemption must go with it.\n48\t */\n49\timport { readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';\n50\timport path from 'node:path';\n51\t\n52\tconst REPO_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n53\tconst REGISTRY_PATH = path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'docs/reviews/_registry/findings.jsonl');\n54\t\n55\t/**\n56\t * A recognised ISO 4217 currency code hardcoded on a currency-writing path:\n57\t *   - a fallback:       `|| 'USD'` / `?? \"nok\"`\n58\t *   - a direct assign:  `currency: 'TRY'`\n59\t *   - a column default: `default: 'USD'`\n60\t * The allowlist keeps the match precise (a bare `[A-Za-z]{3}` would also flag\n61\t * `'ASC'` sort orders and `'UTC'` zones).\n62\t */\n63\tconst CURRENCY_LITERAL =\n64\t  /(?:(?:\\|\\||\\?\\?)\\s*|currency:\\s*|default:\\s*)['\"](TRY|NOK|USD|EUR|GBP|SEK|DKK|CHF|JPY|CNY)['\"]/gi;\n65\t\n66\t/**\n67\t * Currency literals that are allowed to remain, each with the finding that\n68\t * tracks paying them off. NOT an exemption mechanism for new code: adding an\n69\t * entry means registering a finding with an owner and a deadline first.\n70\t */\n71\tconst DECLARED_EXEMPTIONS: ReadonlyArray<{\n72\t  readonly file: string;\n73\t  readonly finding: string;\n74\t  readonly reason: string;\n75\t}> = [\n76\t  // BILLING-MEDIUM-016 — platform-vs-tenant currency is undeclared in billing.\n77\t  // Not asserted to be a defect: Stripe-facing platform billing in USD is a\n78\t  // defensible policy. The defect is that nothing says which reading is meant,\n79\t  // so no gate can enforce either. Declaring it is the fix; until then the\n80\t  // exemption makes the ambiguity visible instead of it being the absence of a"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9842d2e2-2674-4c40-84ec-bcd1c4474f20","uuid":"1e519c72-e175-4279-979f-a9477513fcf8","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:28:19.566Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a29dcc8b0754/tests/invariants/finance-currency-ssot.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * Finance currency SSoT invariant (FARM-MEDIUM-145, FARM-HIGH-151, HR-HIGH-008).\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * The tenant default currency has exactly ONE source of truth:\n *   - farm-service: `finance_settings.defaultCurrency`, resolved through\n *     `FinanceSettingsService`;\n *   - hr-service: `hr_payroll_cost_settings.defaultCurrency`, resolved\n *     through `PayrollCostSettingsService` (itself projected from the\n *     farm SSoT via the `FinanceSettingsUpdated` event).\n *\n * The bug this freezes: independent hardcoded ISO-currency literals drift.\n * `feeds`/`equipment` defaulted to `'TRY'`, `create-feeding-record.handler.ts`\n * to `'NOK'`, HR `employee.entity.ts` to `'USD'` — so a tenant recording feed\n * in one form and equipment in another silently booked in different currencies.\n *\n * # Why this is a rule and not a list\n *\n * It used to guard `FINANCE_HANDLER_ROOTS` recursively PLUS a hardcoded\n * `NAMED_GUARDED_FILES` array, so a currency writer outside those roots was\n * covered only if somebody remembered to add it. Nobody had to: the docblock\n * claimed \"there is no longer a hardcoded-currency create-handler outside this\n * guarded set\" and running the spec's own pattern over every\n * `apps/**\\/*.handler.ts` found SEVEN — two hr payroll handlers (HR-HIGH-008,\n * a real defect: `|| 'USD'` against a platform default of NOK, stamped onto\n * the cross-service `PayrollProcessed` event) and five billing handlers.\n * A ratchet whose coverage is a memo is not a ratchet.\n *\n * So the guard now scans EVERY handler and EVERY entity, and a currency\n * literal is a failure unless it appears in {@link DECLARED_EXEMPTIONS} with a\n * ledger finding behind it. The exemption list can only shrink: a stale entry\n * (declared but no longer matching) fails, so removing the literal forces\n * removing the entry rather than leaving a permanent hole.\n *\n * # When this spec fails\n *\n *   1. A handler or entity grew a `|| 'XXX'` / `?? 'XXX'` / `currency: 'XXX'`\n *      / `default: 'XXX'` currency literal. Resolve it through\n *      `FinanceSettingsService.getDefaultCurrencyInTx(...)` /\n *      `PayrollCostSettingsService.getDefaultCurrencyInTx(...)` instead. For a\n *      column default there is no correct literal at all — a default cannot\n *      know the tenant — so drop it and let the NOT NULL column force the\n *      writer to supply one (precedent: migration 1802200000000).\n *   2. A declared exemption no longer matches. Delete the entry; the debt it\n *      recorded is paid.\n *   3. An exemption cites a finding the ledger has RESOLVED. The finding says\n *      the work is done, so the exemption must go with it.\n */\nimport { readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport path from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\nconst REGISTRY_PATH = path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'docs/reviews/_registry/findings.jsonl');\n\n/**\n * A recognised ISO 4217 currency code hardcoded on a currency-writing path:\n *   - a fallback:       `|| 'USD'` / `?? \"nok\"`\n *   - a direct assign:  `currency: 'TRY'`\n *   - a column default: `default: 'USD'`\n * The allowlist keeps the match precise (a bare `[A-Za-z]{3}` would also flag\n * `'ASC'` sort orders and `'UTC'` zones).\n */\nconst CURRENCY_LITERAL =\n  /(?:(?:\\|\\||\\?\\?)\\s*|currency:\\s*|default:\\s*)['\"](TRY|NOK|USD|EUR|GBP|SEK|DKK|CHF|JPY|CNY)['\"]/gi;\n\n/**\n * Currency literals that are allowed to remain, each with the finding that\n * tracks paying them off. NOT an exemption mechanism for new code: adding an\n * entry means registering a finding with an owner and a deadline first.\n */\nconst DECLARED_EXEMPTIONS: ReadonlyArray<{\n  readonly file: string;\n  readonly finding: string;\n  readonly reason: string;\n}> = [\n  // BILLING-MEDIUM-016 — platform-vs-tenant currency is undeclared in billing.\n  // Not asserted to be a defect: Stripe-facing platform billing in USD is a\n  // defensible policy. The defect is that nothing says which reading is meant,\n  // so no gate can enforce either. Declaring it is the fix; until then the\n  // exemption makes the ambiguity visible instead of it being the absence of a","numLines":80,"startLine":1,"totalLines":222}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FLSYpTfLR4HAU6RFxpeyK7","type":"tool_result","content":"360\t/**\n361\t * Schemas whose owning services run in schema-per-tenant mode (ADR-011 +\n362\t * libs/backend-common/.../tenant-aware-schemas.ts SSoT). Every such\n363\t * source schema MUST have RLS policies registered against its tables —\n364\t * otherwise the tenant_<uuid> clones inherit ZERO policies via\n365\t * CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL and a row-leakage hole opens.\n366\t *\n367\t * Asserted by Part A's RLS-policy probe (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb)\n368\t * and Part B B.5's TENANT_SCOPED audit. Both consumers import this\n369\t * constant via the SERVICES manifest.\n370\t */\n371\tconst RLS_REQUIRED_SCHEMAS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n372\t  'farm',\n373\t  'sensor',\n374\t  'hr',\n375\t  'messaging',\n376\t  'hydroponics',\n377\t  'ai',\n378\t  'alert',\n379\t]);\n380\t\n381\t/**\n382\t * Schemas that MUST exist after the init scripts have run, BEFORE any\n383\t * TypeORM migration touches the DB. These are created exclusively by\n384\t * `00-init-schemas.sh` in the init-scripts bind mount.\n385\t */\n386\tconst REQUIRED_SCHEMAS = [\n387\t  'auth',\n388\t  'farm',\n389\t  'sensor',\n390\t  'hr',\n391\t  'messaging',\n392\t  'hydroponics',\n393\t  'alert',\n394\t  'billing',\n395\t  'notification',\n396\t  'ai',\n397\t  'admin',\n398\t  'observability',\n399\t  'event_store',\n400\t  'shared',\n401\t  'gateway',\n402\t];\n403\t\n404\t/**\n405\t * Critical columns on `auth.users` that the previous regression class\n406\t * silently dropped. Every such\n * source schema MUST have RLS policies registered against its tables —\n * otherwise the tenant_<uuid> clones inherit ZERO policies via\n * CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL and a row-leakage hole opens.\n *\n * Asserted by Part A's RLS-policy probe (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb)\n * and Part B B.5's TENANT_SCOPED audit. Both consumers import this\n * constant via the SERVICES manifest.\n */\nconst RLS_REQUIRED_SCHEMAS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n  'farm',\n  'sensor',\n  'hr',\n  'messaging',\n  'hydroponics',\n  'ai',\n  'alert',\n]);\n\n/**\n * Schemas that MUST exist after the init scripts have run, BEFORE any\n * TypeORM migration touches the DB. These are created exclusively by\n * `00-init-schemas.sh` in the init-scripts bind mount.\n */\nconst REQUIRED_SCHEMAS = [\n  'auth',\n  'farm',\n  'sensor',\n  'hr',\n  'messaging',\n  'hydroponics',\n  'alert',\n  'billing',\n  'notification',\n  'ai',\n  'admin',\n  'observability',\n  'event_store',\n  'shared',\n  'gateway',\n];\n\n/**\n * Critical columns on `auth.users` that the previous regression class\n * silently dropped. Each one was added by `1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema`\n * after the squash damage was identified — proving they exist after a\n * fresh-volume init is the exact failure mode this test guards.\n */","numLines":50,"startLine":360,"totalLines":1416}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KePhBDMXoitC9QpkYbDXZ7","type":"tool_result","content":"255\t\n256\t  # ===================================================================\n257\t  # bootstrap-from-scratch — fresh-volume integration check.\n258\t  #\n259\t  # Runs the apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts test,\n260\t  # which spins up an empty Postgres testcontainer with the production\n261\t  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n262\t  # against it, and asserts the resulting schema state is what the\n263\t  # current entity surface expects.\n264\t  #\n265\t  # Why a separate job: this one needs a Docker daemon and ~5 minutes;\n266\t  # the conventions checks above are pure-shell <30s. Splitting keeps\n267\t  # PR feedback fast for the 99% case (filename/class-name regressions)\n268\t  # while still gating the rarer-but-catastrophic squash regression.\n269\t  #\n270\t  # GHA runners have docker preinstalled and the testcontainers library\n271\t  # autoreaches it; no extra setup beyond `npm ci`.\n272\t  # ===================================================================\n273\t  bootstrap-from-scratch:\n274\t    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n275\t    # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — bootstrap-from-scratch is now ~8 minutes\n276\t    # because the entity-surface coverage matrix runs per service AND\n277\t    # the SchemaDriftValidator runtime gate (Part A.4) iterates each\n278\t    # service's metadata. 12-minute ceiling gives ~50% headroom while\n279\t    # keeping the zombie-job blast radius bounded.\n280\t    timeout-minutes: 12\n281\t\n282\t    steps:\n283\t      - name: Checkout repository\n284\t        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n285\t\n286\t      - name: Setup Node.js\n287\t        uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n288\t        with:\n289\t          node-version: '22'\n290\t          cache: 'npm'\n291\t\n292\t      - name: Install dependencies\n293\t        run: npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --ignore-scripts --no-fund\n294\t\n295\t      # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — testcontainers image cache extension.\n296\t      # The Postgres pull (~250 MB compressed) dominates cold-start\n297\t      # time; restoring the image tarball from a cache key shaves\n298\t      # 60-90 seconds off every CI run that touches a path-filtered\n299\t      # surface. Cache key bound to the pinned digest in the spec\n300\t      # so a digest bump invalidates the cache (correct behaviour).\n301\t      - name: Restore docker image cache\n302\t        id: docker-image-cache\n303\t        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6.1.0\n304\t        with:\n305\t          path: /tmp/docker-image-cache\n306\t          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n307\t\n308\t      - name: Load cached image (if present)\n309\t        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit == 'true'\n310\t        run: |\n311\t          if [ -f /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar ]; then\n312\t            docker load -i /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar\n313\t          fi\n314\t\n315\t      - name: Run bootstrap-from-scratch test\n316\t        run: npm run test:bootstrap\n317\t        env:\n318\t          # testcontainers reuse cuts seconds off subsequent runs and is\n319\t          # safe in single-job CI (we don't reuse across jobs).\n320\t          TESTCONTAINERS_REUSE_ENABLE: 'true'\n321\t\n322\t      - name: Save docker image cache\n323\t        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'\n324\t        run: |\n325\t          # Best-effort: save the timescaledb-ha image to the cache for\n326\t          # future runs. The grep|head pipeline can return empty if the\n327\t          # testcontainer cleaned up before this step runs (race), or\n328\t          # produce an `<none>:<none>` tag when an image is referenced\n329\t          # only by digest. Both yield `docker save` \"invalid reference\n330\t          # format\" and a non-zero exit code that fails the whole job.\n331\t          # The cache is an optimization, not a correctness gate — the\n332\t          # bootstrap test has already completed successfully by the\n333\t          # time this step runs. Guard the docker invocation so a cache\n334\t          # miss-save degrades to a no-op instead of failing CI.\n335\t          mkdir -p /tmp/docker-image-cache\n336\t          IMAGE_REF=$(docker images --format '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}' \\\n337\t            | grep timescaledb-ha \\\n338\t            | grep -v '<none>' \\\n339\t            | head -1)\n340\t          if [ -z \"$IMAGE_REF\" ]; then\n341\t            echo \"No timescaledb-ha image with a usable tag found; skipping cache save.\"\n342\t            exit 0\n343\t          fi\n344\t          docker save -o /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar \"$IMAGE_REF\" \\\n345\t            || echo \"docker save failed for $IMAGE_REF; cache will be regenerated on next run.\"\n346\t\n347\t  # ===================================================================\n348\t  # tenant-clone-parity — Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 Part C.\n349\t  #\n350\t  # Asserts that per-tenant schema clones are a structurally faithful,\n351\t  # RLS-enabled mirror of every source-schema table. Runs against a\n352\t  # live Postgres service container (NOT testcontainers) after applying\n353\t  # the same aqua-db-migrate entrypoint used by production deploys.\n354\t  #"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9842d2e2-2674-4c40-84ec-bcd1c4474f20","uuid":"a5ec8c56-3fae-4f26-a5cd-dd9d407bbf5c","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:28:58.360Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a29dcc8b0754/.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml","content":"\n  # ===================================================================\n  # bootstrap-from-scratch — fresh-volume integration check.\n  #\n  # Runs the apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts test,\n  # which spins up an empty Postgres testcontainer with the production\n  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n  # against it, and asserts the resulting schema state is what the\n  # current entity surface expects.\n  #\n  # Why a separate job: this one needs a Docker daemon and ~5 minutes;\n  # the conventions checks above are pure-shell <30s. Splitting keeps\n  # PR feedback fast for the 99% case (filename/class-name regressions)\n  # while still gating the rarer-but-catastrophic squash regression.\n  #\n  # GHA runners have docker preinstalled and the testcontainers library\n  # autoreaches it; no extra setup beyond `npm ci`.\n  # ===================================================================\n  bootstrap-from-scratch:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — bootstrap-from-scratch is now ~8 minutes\n    # because the entity-surface coverage matrix runs per service AND\n    # the SchemaDriftValidator runtime gate (Part A.4) iterates each\n    # service's metadata. 12-minute ceiling gives ~50% headroom while\n    # keeping the zombie-job blast radius bounded.\n    timeout-minutes: 12\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout repository\n        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n\n      - name: Setup Node.js\n        uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n          cache: 'npm'\n\n      - name: Install dependencies\n        run: npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --ignore-scripts --no-fund\n\n      # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — testcontainers image cache extension.\n      # The Postgres pull (~250 MB compressed) dominates cold-start\n      # time; restoring the image tarball from a cache key shaves\n      # 60-90 seconds off every CI run that touches a path-filtered\n      # surface. Cache key bound to the pinned digest in the spec\n      # so a digest bump invalidates the cache (correct behaviour).\n      - name: Restore docker image cache\n        id: docker-image-cache\n        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6.1.0\n        with:\n          path: /tmp/docker-image-cache\n          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n\n      - name: Load cached image (if present)\n        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit == 'true'\n        run: |\n          if [ -f /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar ]; then\n            docker load -i /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar\n          fi\n\n      - name: Run bootstrap-from-scratch test\n        run: npm run test:bootstrap\n        env:\n          # testcontainers reuse cuts seconds off subsequent runs and is\n          # safe in single-job CI (we don't reuse across jobs).\n          TESTCONTAINERS_REUSE_ENABLE: 'true'\n\n      - name: Save docker image cache\n        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'\n        run: |\n          # Best-effort: save the timescaledb-ha image to the cache for\n          # future runs. The grep|head pipeline can return empty if the\n          # testcontainer cleaned up before this step runs (race), or\n          # produce an `<none>:<none>` tag when an image is referenced\n          # only by digest. Both yield `docker save` \"invalid reference\n          # format\" and a non-zero exit code that fails the whole job.\n          # The cache is an optimization, not a correctness gate — the\n          # bootstrap test has already completed successfully by the\n          # time this step runs. Guard the docker invocation so a cache\n          # miss-save degrades to a no-op instead of failing CI.\n          mkdir -p /tmp/docker-image-cache\n          IMAGE_REF=$(docker images --format '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}' \\\n            | grep timescaledb-ha \\\n            | grep -v '<none>' \\\n            | head -1)\n          if [ -z \"$IMAGE_REF\" ]; then\n            echo \"No timescaledb-ha image with a usable tag found; skipping cache save.\"\n            exit 0\n          fi\n          docker save -o /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar \"$IMAGE_REF\" \\\n            || echo \"docker save failed for $IMAGE_REF; cache will be regenerated on next run.\"\n\n  # ===================================================================\n  # tenant-clone-parity — Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 Part C.\n  #\n  # Asserts that per-tenant schema clones are a structurally faithful,\n  # RLS-enabled mirror of every source-schema table. Runs against a\n  # live Postgres service container (NOT testcontainers) after applying\n  # the same aqua-db-migrate entrypoint used by production deploys.\n  #","numLines":100,"startLine":255,"totalLines":777}}}
